**The date-line check on this volume is a different instrument from every other range's and it is the ninth time this repository has walked into a check that fails silently.** All ten files open their date line with **`This is the`** and `tools/measure.py`'s own `DATE_LINE` requires **`It is the`**, so the whole-formula locator printed in every prompt in this repair **returns zero lines on all ten** — measured, not inherited. The locator is the either-opener formula `grep -nE "^(This|It) is the .* day of the .* week of the .* month of the year after"`, which returned exactly one line in each file, on **9 9 7 7 9 7 7 9 7 7**. **`sed -n '5p'` tests a date line in no file of this range, measured on all ten**, and **`calendar --volume 08` parses 26 of 50 files.** The volume census is **26 opening `It is the`, 22 opening `This is the`, and 2 with neither** — `chapter-0374.md`, whose date sentence opens *"The date is the second day…"*, and `chapter-0379.md`, which has none. **`tools/measure.py` was not edited and was not wrong: its `prose_lines` docstring says it selects date lines by the module's own `DATE_LINE` "and not by a `startswith("It is ")`". A volume that opens half its date lines differently is a fact about the volume.**

**The body-method finding has arrived in a third shape, and the third reason is a chapter heading.** The hand-copied `^It is ` strip removes **no date line at all on this range — 0 of 10, measured** — because no date line here opens `It is `. So its defect cannot fire on a date line in this volume, and the whole of the difference between the two methods is **chapter headings, which the strip keeps and `prose_lines` drops**: three of the ten headings carry a demonstrative-plus-noun, *that stair* in `chapter-0353.md`, *that goes* in `chapter-0355.md`, *that went* in `chapter-0359.md`. **The strip reads 207 and the page reads 206 on the landed sweep, and both are printed, and the page is the figure of record.** `grep -c 're\.sub(r"\^It is\.\*\$"' state/batch-summary.md` returns **13** and was **13** before this phase.

**Forty-nine items in two independent reads, and not one was visible to any instrument in this repository.** `subagent_type: novel-reviewer`, given the classes of defect and no list of things to write, returned **thirty-eight findings on the first pass**. **Three were hard defects of the simplest kind**: a doubled conjunction produced by a rewording pass, a possessive with its noun missing, and a pronoun with no antecedent beside a "second one" with nothing to be second. **The second read, on the corrected prose, found eleven more, and the worst defect this pass introduced anywhere was created by its own first correction**: removing the unsupported count *twice* from an added sentence left it reading **"She had said all of it out loud in this building and had been thanked for it"**, against `chapter-0356.md`'s own line 31 *"Nobody thanked her for saying it"* and against the volume's standing lock. **A correction that removes half a sentence and leaves behind the half that breaks the volume's lock is the ninth range on which a correction has been prose, and the first on which the damage was a lock and not a repetition.** The same read caught **invented daylight in a window four chapters say has been broken, a fee board moved from behind a woman to in front of her, and a woman's arrival at the bottom of four steps reversed against the paragraph above it.** All fixed. **`lifts` is blind to all of it and reading is the only instrument this repository has that looks at what a chapter says.**

**The instrument gap is now three-deep and the third index earned its place again.** **Six runs at eight words of added prose against the range's own ORIGINAL text were found by the third index and five were revoiced**; the sixth turned out to be the one whose holder is **`chapter-0603.md`, whose matching line is absent from that file at `e30e228` and was written by `prose-repair-0002`.** **That is item 173Q's standing arriving on a chapter nobody had opened: ask whether a holder is ORIGINAL before you ask which month it is. ### Item 173V — the re-audit of `chapter-0351.md` to `chapter-0360.md` found eighteen defects in a completed repair, three of them hard, and one of that record's claims about its own method was false

**A repair phase was dispatched a second time on the first range of Volume 08 and found the work done.** `git rev-parse HEAD` returned **`814da5e24407227a1c64ab305816dff5ecfbbdb6`** and the ten files stood at **19,679 words against the prompt's recorded pre-repair 15,506**, and `git log --oneline ec83e35..HEAD -- chapters/volume-08/` returned **one commit, `05f9c27 novel: save writer work prose-repair-0013`**, which had added ten to fourteen lines to every one of them. **The range's real pre-repair base is `4542aea641d1d0fbf1002bf4017cd0ec4dff2276` and it reproduces the prompt's table to the word.** This phase audited and did not rewrite, and it fixed what the audit found in the repair's own added prose.

**Eighteen defects, three of them hard, and not one would have passed any instrument in this repository.**

* **`chapter-0356.md` had the bench in two states at once.** One added paragraph said it *"was occupied from one end of it to the other"* and another, seventy-four lines below, said it was *"warm where the men had been sitting at it and **cold at the ends of it**"* — **and the second also contradicted the chapter's own closing line, which has nine men still in the bay at the sixth hour.**
* **`chapter-0353.md` had a sentence that counted three things and then divided them into two.** *"She said the three of them … One of them was on the board of the column she had open and the other was along the edge of the counter"* — **four quoted speeches follow, and the only candidate antecedent on the page is the three copies that left the room twenty lines earlier.**
* **`chapter-0358.md` gave a corridor opening hours the same chapter denies**, in *"Four hours is what a counter is open for and four hours is what a passage is"*, against its own date line's *"the racks go down all day"* and its own *"the last rack of the day"*.
* **`chapter-0353.md` had dust settling on the lid of a box the page puts under a counter.** **This manuscript does have dust on the lid of a box under a work surface — `chapter-0462.md` — but there the boards are lifted every working day and this counter is not.**
* **`chapter-0360.md` had an added paragraph opening on `It is `, and that is the shape the strip helper deletes** — see the method finding below.
* **`chapter-0355.md` had a pronoun with no antecedent and one clause printed twice inside a single sentence**, *"does not look at the middle of it"* twenty words from itself.
* **`chapter-0353.md` had *"has not decided whether he"* three times in twenty lines**, once with no complement at all, against the original line 17's *"has not decided whether he has come to the right counter."*
* **`chapter-0353.md` gave the speech a second addressee** the same file had already fixed as *"the man and the stair."*
* **Nine restatements and same-act pairs** across `chapter-0351.md`, `0352.md`, `0354.md`, `0355.md`, `0357.md`, `0358.md`, `0359.md` and `0360.md`: a broken window that gives nothing twice in one chapter; an added paragraph opening by restating the paragraph above it; a near end with a lifted corner twice; **one clause stated three times in seventy-nine lines**; an added paragraph restating the original's image and then misusing a pronoun; two added paragraphs forty-six lines apart doing one act; an added paragraph restating the preamble and another re-narrating a neighbour's beats in order; **two added inventories thirty-six lines apart, one of which said *"she would not be able to name a fourth"* against the other's list of four things she has**; and a generalisation standing where an action belonged.

**The record's claim about its own method was false, and it is a certificate rather than a sentence, which is the class no command here can see.** The earlier record on this range states that the `^It is ` strip's defect *"cannot fire"* on this volume and that the difference between the two body methods is *"entirely chapter headings."* **The first half is right — no date line in this range opens `It is ` — and the second half is wrong.** `grep -rn "^It is " chapters/volume-08/chapter-03[56][0-9].md` returned **three lines** as the repair delivered this range, and the strip **deletes three whole prose paragraphs, 175 words, not three headings**: `chapter-0352.md:71` (47 words, base), `chapter-0357.md:21` (37 words, base), and `chapter-0360.md:37` (**91 words, and it was added prose the repair itself had written**). Measured on a detached worktree at `814da5e`, the sweep read **208 on the page and 210 under the strip**, and 208 plus the three headings the strip keeps — `that stair`, `that goes`, `that went`, one demonstrative-plus-noun each — minus the three paragraphs it destroys is 210. **A method that deletes a repair's own paragraph while certifying that it destroys nothing is the tenth instrument finding of this shape and the first one in which the destroyed prose is the prose under audit.** The range now stands at **two** `^It is ` openers, both base, 84 words, and the sweep at **209 and 212** with the difference of three accounted for by the three headings alone — because the two paragraphs the strip destroys carry none of the counted bigrams between them, which was measured and is why the arithmetic closes to the digit.

**Two figures in that earlier record are out and are corrected here rather than in place, because a superseded claim a reader can check is worth more than a clean file.** It certifies **"four instances"** of the clipped register *"shy it / right it / position it"* across two chapters; `grep -rn "shy it\|right it\|position it" chapters/volume-08/` returns **three**. It certifies **"12 chapters across 5 volumes"** holding *"the foot of about nine hundred sheets a year"*; the exact string in the token stream is held by **11 chapters across 5 volumes**, ten of them ORIGINAL and one — `chapter-0357.md` — repair-written. **Both conclusions the earlier record drew still hold on the corrected figures.** A third figure is wrong in a way that matters more and is section One of the record.

**One canon tension nobody had reported, found here and not settled.** `chapter-0356.md`'s own line 5 says **"She is fifty-one"** and `chapter-0357.md`'s own line 23 says **"a clerk of about fifty-five with a pen on a table."** Both base at `4542aea`. **Four years is not this manuscript's register for one person in two chapters and no phase may decide which is right.**

**The regression standing held for the fourteenth range running, and this phase paid for it five times.** **The named lists rose from 33 to 34 and from 46 and 51 to 47 and 52, caused by this phase's own revoicing of `chapter-0355.md` introducing `on that table`;** caught by re-measuring after the pass and fixed. **Lifts of seven words or more were created by this phase's revoicing, one of them at nine words** — *"has been on that wall longer than she has"*, which was the direct product of this phase's own first attempt at the bench fix. **A seven-word echo between two chapters, *"grey with the dust that comes off"*, was the direct product of this phase's dust fix landing next to an existing line in `chapter-0358.md`, and a six-word echo, *"She said the whole of it"*, was the product of its revoicing of `chapter-0353.md`.** **The six-word echo was invisible at the seven-word floor and was found only because the added-against-added index was run at six, which is the standing, now twice on this range.** Final state flat at **33 / 46 / 51**. **Not one word of original prose was removed.** Line-level diff against `4542aea` over the ten files: **122 `insert`, 734 `equal`, 0 `delete`, 0 `replace` in lines**, and **inserted-plus-equal-equals-the-line-total holds on every file, 122 + 734 = 856 and the ten files hold 856 lines.** **The same diff returns 71 `equal` and 61 `insert` opcode blocks, and this record printed those two numbers under the label *line-level*, which is how a third pass came to copy 71 into a sentence counting lines; both units are named here so that neither is read as the other.** **The eighty-one lines this phase's own word-level diff against `814da5e` reports as changed were intersected with the lines the pre-repair commit holds, and the intersection is empty.** All ten date lines byte-identical to `814da5e` **and on the line numbers they held**, located by the either-opener formula, because **line 5 is a date line in no file of this range**; all ten titles byte-identical; section breaks **6 6 5 6 5 6 6 4 6 5**; question marks **zero**; bold markers **8 10 14 10 16 12 16 14 12 14**, unchanged, and this phase added no interiority; trailing newlines ten of ten. **All ten files' quoted spans are byte-identical to `814da5e` — ten IDENTICAL and zero CHANGED**, run as a command. **Nobody was thanked, forgiven or sent for, nothing was resolved, the unmade decision about the woman of twenty-four is unmade in both directions, and `chapter-0364.md`'s one question mark is base text outside this range.**

**A standing for Volume 08 that is now nine findings deep and is transferable: on this volume the located-date-line check is the only date check that sees anything.** Every prompt in this repair prints `grep -n "^It is the …"`, which returns **nothing at all** in all ten of this range's files and exits zero, and every check built on it reports clean. **The four findings, all in state files this phase wrote or refreshed beside the prose.**

* **A duplicated paragraph tail in `state/current.md`, twice over.** The third pass replaced the first half of two paragraphs and left the old tail standing, so a sentence about the scope decision appeared twice on line 92 and *"one hundred and forty chapters carrying the same defect"* twice inside four lines, and the same shape was on line 34 where the stale text survived a `****`. **This is the class the repair exists to remove, and it was in the repair's own record of itself.** Both tails are out and no sentence over sixty characters now appears twice in that file.
* **Two figures that do not reproduce, and one of them had been travelling.** The word-level total printed **3,869** where 19,374 minus 15,506 gives **3,868**, and the error is located in the per-file column beside it, which printed `chapter-0358.md` at 418 where the file gives 417. And both records printed a line-level `equal` of **71**, which is this diff's equal **opcode block** count and not its line count of **734**. **The 71 and the 61 belong to item 173V and are correct under the label that record prints them with; a later record copied the opcode number into a sentence counting lines, and that is how one wrong figure became two.** All four places carrying either figure are corrected in place, and item 173V's two records now name both units. **Item 173K's standing — a figure that is one out is corrected where it stood — held for the fourth time on this range, and this time the wrong figure was one a record about figures had itself printed.**
* **A garbled bold run and an arithmetic impossibility in `state/current.md:36`.** The added paragraph ended in an empty bold run and left the paragraph's bold open, and two other paragraphs in the file carried an unclosed run or a stray marker, **none of which changes a word and all of which change what a reader sees.** Every paragraph in that file now has balanced bold, checked by counting with inline code spans excluded. **The same paragraph claimed that "twenty-four of Volume 08's fifty chapters have now had three passes and the other thirty have had none" — 54 of 50, and it contradicts the ten chapters of Volume 08 repaired at all. The true statement is that 0351 to 0360 are the only ten chapters of that volume any pass has opened for edit, so those ten have had three passes each and the other forty have had none**, and `state/current.md`, this item and the batch summary now say that.
* **A prompt that disagreed with itself about what is left.** `workspace/prose-repair-0014/PROMPT.md` carried a hundred and forty chapters at twenty-eight per cent in its body, a hundred and twenty at twenty-four in its closing line and a hundred and thirty at twenty-six in between. **All three now read off one arithmetic — 128 repaired and 492 unrepaired today, 138 and 482 after that phase, 130 unrepaired in the three untouched places and 130 over 482 is twenty-seven per cent** — and the phase estimate is stated the same way, forty-nine across the manuscript and thirteen across those three places. Its heading said fifteen ranges running while two sentences in its body still said thirteen; all three say fifteen.

**The fifth, found by re-deriving what the review named, and it is the one worth keeping. The review accepted the prompt's self-correction of its sweep figure and the self-correction was backwards.** The prompt had replaced **141 = 12.58 across 77 forms** with **139 = 12.40 across 76** and said the earlier figure came from an exclusion set that had grown. Re-run with the `NONNOUN` set of record pasted whole, **the exclusion set is identical in both runs and is not the cause: `m.prose_lines` gives 141 and the `body_strip` helper gives 139, and the difference is two hits on one form, `those books`, because `re.sub(r"^It is.*$", "", t, flags=re.M)` deletes two lines of prose and not two date lines at `chapter-0368.md:3` and `chapter-0370.md:3`.** **So 141 counts the prose and 139 loses two lines of it, and the prompt had adopted the second while naming the first the error.** The prompt now prints both, names the cause and says which to quote, and item 173W's next-range paragraph carries both beside it so the two records agree. **One further figure in that prompt did not hold either: on 0371 to 0380 the closed list of ten is 7 at 0.62 per 1,000 with five of the ten carrying none and not four, and the words and sweep for that range are now printed with it, 11,365 words at mean 1,136 and a sweep of 110 = 9.68 across 67 forms.**

**The standing this hands on is one item 173Q already prints in its sharpest form: print the unit, and print both units when a method returns more than one.** Two of the review's four findings were figures that named a method and printed a number the method did not return, and **neither would have been caught by re-running the method, because both reproduce under the method printed beside them once the unit is read as well as the number.** And the fifth is the failure this repair has been built against for twenty ranges, happening inside the machinery built to catch it: **a figure that failed to reproduce was investigated and the investigation found the figure right and the correction wrong**, because the explanation for why it had moved was believed instead of the selector that deletes prose.

**What is left is most of it and no phase may imply otherwise, and this phase moved none of it. One hundred and twenty-eight chapters of six hundred and twenty have been through the prose repair, twenty-one per cent, and this phase repaired none: it corrected four findings in three state files and one prompt and touched no prose at all.** Volume 11 is repaired in full from 0501 to 0550 and Volume 12 in full from 0551 to 0620, and both are untouched nowhere. **Volume 08 is repaired from 0351 to 0360 and those ten chapters are the only ones of its fifty any pass has opened for edit.** **Volumes 09 and 10 are untouched in their entirety and Volume 08 is untouched from 0361 to 0400 — a hundred and forty chapters, twenty-eight per cent of what is left, and still the single largest block of this defect in the repository. Four hundred and ninety-two chapters are unrepaired, seventy-nine per cent.** **Two passes over ten chapters have now produced four findings in the records and none in the prose, and that is the argument for repairing forward rather than auditing back.**

**The next ten are `chapter-0361.md` to `chapter-0370.md`, still `--volume 08`, and the prompt for them is `workspace/prose-repair-0014/PROMPT.md`, which this phase corrected in place rather than duplicating.** Its figures re-measured on this tree: **11,208 words, mean 1,121; 26 closed-list hits at 2.32 with `that room` 13 leading and `that bay` 9 second; twenty-three 47 = 4.19; twenty-five 49 = 4.37; sweep 141 = 12.58 across 77 forms on `m.prose_lines` and 139 = 12.40 across 76 on `body_strip`; and `chapter-0364.md`'s one base question mark at line 25, which a repair may not remove.** **The standing for that phase is the one above: measure the sweep on `m.prose_lines`, print the figure the strip gives beside it, and name which of the two deletes prose.**

**Four debts are outstanding and no pass in this repair touched them, and they are owed elsewhere and not here.**
